		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=53086</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Learn how AI cost management helps organizations move from AI pilots to measurable ROI through greater visibility, governance, and optimization.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-economics-of-agent-optimization-from-pilots-to-measurable-returns/">The Economics of Agent Optimization: From pilots to measurable returns</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><em>This blog post is the first of a four-part series called <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/tag/the-economics-of-agent-optimization/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">The Economics of Agent Optimization</a> which shares the strategies, capabilities, and proof points to help you optimize agent costs and run AI as a managed investment system on Microsoft Foundry.</em></p>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The AI conversation in most enterprises has moved from the whiteboard to the budget review. Two years ago, the question was whether AI could work. The question leaders are asking now is sharper and less comfortable: is it paying for itself? </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For the teams now in production—including more than 100,000 organizations <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">building on Microsoft Foundry</a> that question has become urgent. Tokens have become the new unit of technology spend, and financial discipline (not model choice) is what decides whether a promising pilot ever scales. The money is already moving in: in a <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2025/11/11/bridging-the-ai-divide-how-frontier-firms-are-transforming-business/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Microsoft-commissioned IDC study</a> of more than 4,000 business leaders, 71% said they plan to increase AI budgets, funded from IT and non-IT sources alike. The budgets are growing. The question is whether the discipline grows with them.&nbsp;</p>



<figure class="wp-block-pullquote has-electric-blue-300-color has-text-color has-link-color wp-elements-2614fd590e882918aba662d7868b0f7c" style="border-top-left-radius:0px;border-top-right-radius:0px;border-bottom-left-radius:0px;border-bottom-right-radius:0px;padding-top:var(--wp--preset--spacing--md);padding-bottom:var(--wp--preset--spacing--md)"><blockquote><p><strong><em>71% of business leaders plan to increase their AI budgets</em></strong></p><cite>2025 IDC survey</cite></blockquote></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The teams pulling ahead did not go looking for a cheaper model. They stopped running AI as a string of one-off pilots and started running it as a managed investment system: every request sized to its job, every agent improved as it runs, and every dollar bounded and accounted for. That shift, from buying intelligence to managing it, is the whole game. This series is about how the system works and why Microsoft Foundry is built to run it.
</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Start building on Microsoft Foundry</a></div>
</div>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="understand-your-ai-costs-and-spending">Understand your AI costs and spending</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Before you can manage AI spend, you need to understand what creates it. Cost is not determined only by the model you choose. It is also shaped by the application or agent built around that model.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Every request includes input tokens, such as system prompts, conversation history, tool definitions, and retrieved content, as well as output tokens generated by the model. Because models are stateless, the full context is sent with every request. Costs can increase over time even when the user asks only a simple follow-up question.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Agents introduce another layer of complexity. Instead of following a single path, an agent may evaluate options, retry actions, or call multiple tools before producing a response. A single user request can generate many model calls, making workflow design as important as model selection.
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="improve-ai-cost-visibility-across-teams">Improve AI cost visibility across teams</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  AI spend is difficult to manage when it appears as a single aggregate number. Teams need visibility into costs by application, agent, workflow, and model to understand what is driving usage and where optimization opportunities exist.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Without that level of attribution, it becomes difficult to explain costs, prioritize improvements, or measure the impact of optimization efforts.
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="control-and-optimize-spend">Control and optimize spend</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Visibility alone is not enough. AI workloads can scale quickly, and unexpected behavior can increase consumption in a short period of time. Organizations need controls that help manage spend before costs become a surprise.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Optimization also requires more than selecting a lower-cost model. Most AI workloads contain a mix of requests with different requirements. Better outcomes come from matching requests to the right models, reducing unnecessary context, limiting unneeded tool use, and improving agent workflows so they operate more efficiently.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="why-microsoft-is-the-platform-for-ai-finops">Why Microsoft is the platform for AI FinOps</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">FinOps began as the discipline of bringing financial accountability to variable cloud&nbsp;spend,&nbsp;a shared operating model that puts engineering, finance, and product on one set of numbers. FinOps for AI comes down to four commitments:</p>



<ol class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Make AI predictable to fund</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Efficient by design</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Optimized&nbsp;at scale</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Proven in value&nbsp;</li>
</ol>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft&#8217;s answer is a single, first-party approach to FinOps for AI that spans the entire lifecycle—plan, build, manage, and measure. Cost visibility and control are built into the products teams already use: Microsoft Foundry and GitHub where agents are built and run, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/cost-management/?ef_id=_k_Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlTvgPpAWE0VF6esP6uE_g62gdg7V-YSoF9em5d7AwVduHjni5qiVQ4aAoFOEALw_wcB_k_&amp;OCID=AIDcmm2gz5ejpc_SEM__k_Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlTvgPpAWE0VF6esP6uE_g62gdg7V-YSoF9em5d7AwVduHjni5qiVQ4aAoFOEALw_wcB_k_&amp;gad_source=1&amp;gad_campaignid=21496728177&amp;gbraid=0AAAAADcJh_sTdbGVz4yQLKUFm2AuSzSJ6&amp;gclid=Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlTvgPpAWE0VF6esP6uE_g62gdg7V-YSoF9em5d7AwVduHjni5qiVQ4aAoFOEALw_wcB" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Cost Management</a> for allocation and chargeback, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/?ef_id=_k_Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlRNGEoqyHl1k5hCpZA5Zn48ALL5otVcT5jfO5HLFk5a-a4XtWbf1mMaAo9qEALw_wcB_k_&amp;OCID=AIDcmm2gz5ejpc_SEM__k_Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlRNGEoqyHl1k5hCpZA5Zn48ALL5otVcT5jfO5HLFk5a-a4XtWbf1mMaAo9qEALw_wcB_k_&amp;gad_source=1&amp;gad_campaignid=21496728177&amp;gbraid=0AAAAADcJh_sTdbGVz4yQLKUFm2AuSzSJ6&amp;gclid=Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlRNGEoqyHl1k5hCpZA5Zn48ALL5otVcT5jfO5HLFk5a-a4XtWbf1mMaAo9qEALw_wcB" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure pricing</a> offers for commitment-based savings, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/api-management" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure API Management</a> as the gateway that meters and governs AI traffic. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-agent-365" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Agent 365</a> extends the same discipline to the tenant—unifying agent cost management across Microsoft and third-party platforms with spending policies, budget caps, and departmental chargeback in one place. Together they give organizations something no point tool can: comprehensive, best-in-class cost management across the whole AI estate, from the first prompt to the board-level ROI number.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Foundry is where that approach gets specific, because it&#8217;s where agents are run and optimized. It runs AI as a managed investment system across one closed loop: <strong>optimize each request at runtime, optimize each agent workflow over time, and govern the spend continuously</strong>.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="ai-cost-optimization-starts-with-visibility-1">AI cost optimization starts with visibility</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A managed investment system makes three decisions, each at a different speed. You&nbsp;optimize&nbsp;the request&nbsp;in&nbsp;the moment it runs. You optimize the agent workflow over days and weeks, as you learn what works. And you govern the spend continuously, with limits and budgets that never sleep. Foundry is built to make all three. Each move has its own set of Foundry capabilities, and the map below shows how they fit together. </p>



<figure class="wp-block-table is-style-stripes"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th><strong>The decision</strong></th><th><strong>What Foundry gives you</strong><span style="font-size: 14px"></span></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><strong>Optimize the request, at runtime</strong><br><em>Right-size every&nbsp;call&nbsp;so simple work never pays frontier prices.</em><br></td><td><ul><li><strong>Model router for Microsoft Foundry</strong> routes each prompt across cost, quality, and balanced modes, so simple requests don&#8217;t pay frontier-model prices.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Deployment and pricing options</strong>&nbsp;match each workload to its cost and latency needs, spanning Global, Data Zone, and&nbsp;Regional&nbsp;deployments&nbsp;and the Standard, Priority, Provisioned Throughput, and Batch processing modes.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Prompt and semantic caching</strong> reuse repeated context instead of paying to recompute it.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Fine-tuning</strong>&nbsp;lets a smaller tuned model match a larger one on your task, lowering the per-token rate and shortening prompts.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Microsoft IQ </strong>provides a shared enterprise intelligence layer across how people work, how the business operates, institutional knowledge, and the web. Within that layer, Foundry IQ gives agents reusable, permission-aware knowledge bases and uses agentic retrieval to select only the most relevant context. This improves grounding while reducing unnecessary input tokens.</li></ul></td></tr><tr><td><strong>Optimize the workflow, over time</strong><br><em>Make each agent cheaper as it learns what works.</em>&nbsp;<br></td><td><ul><li><strong>Agent optimizer</strong>&nbsp;tests prompts, models, tools, and skills against your own evaluators and promotes the best configuration, often holding quality on a smaller, cheaper model.&nbsp;</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Toolboxes</strong> send only the tools a request needs instead of every definition.&nbsp;</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Memory </strong>(procedural, user, and session memory) carries context across turns without resending the full history.&nbsp;</li></ul></td></tr><tr><td><strong>Govern the spend, continuously </strong><br><em>Set limits and budgets that hold, so no agent can run away with the bill.</em>&nbsp;<br></td><td><ul><li><strong>Azure API Management&#8217;s AI Gateway</strong> can be deployed in front of your Foundry endpoints as a separate AI Gateway layer, applying token rate limits, quotas, and caching for teams that already standardize on Azure API Management. We are working to deliver more seamless and integrated AI Gateway functionalities in Foundry.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Foundry in-platform budgets and enforcement</strong> will be available soon to bring spending limits and enforcement natively into Foundry, closer to where agents run. </li></ul><br><ul><li>Foundry gives you model- and deployment-level cost reporting today, with Azure Cost Management as the system of record for budgets, alerts, and billed costs. Richer attribution, down to the individual agent and session, is on the roadmap. </li></ul></td></tr></tbody></table></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Agent 365 will extend governance to the tenant, unifying cost management across Microsoft and third-party agents with spending policies, budget caps, and departmental chargeback.&nbsp; </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  You can watch the runtime levers work live in our <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mB0IyELzjRg">new Microsoft Mechanics episode on token economics</a>. 
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-four-questions-ai-leaders-should-be-asking">The four questions AI leaders should be asking</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  If you take one thing from this post, take these four questions into your next AI or budget review. Each has a concrete answer in Foundry. If you cannot answer one today, that is where to start.
</p>



<ol class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Do we know what we&#8217;re paying for?</strong><br>Spend should be visible by model, agent, and workflow, not hidden in a single invoice line. Foundry&#8217;s metering and traces make it easier to understand where costs originate.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Are we paying the right amount for each request?</strong><br>Most requests do not need a frontier model. Model router, deployment and pricing options, caching, fine-tuning, and Foundry IQ help match each request to the capability it needs.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Are our agents operating efficiently?</strong><br>Agent costs should improve over time as workflows become more effective. Agent optimizer and memory in <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/agent-service/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry Agent Service</a> and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/how-to/tools/toolbox?pivots=python" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Toolboxes in Foundry</a> help reduce unnecessary token usage and improve execution quality.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Do our limits hold when usage spikes?</strong><br>Usage that expands rapidly needs controls that hold. Today, many teams put Azure API Management in front of their AI endpoints to enforce token rate limits and quotas at the AI Gateway layer. Native budgets and enforcement inside Foundry, plus tenant-wide controls through Agent 365, are where we are headed next.</li>
</ol>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The first question is about understanding AI spend. The next three are the areas this series explores in more detail: matching requests to the right models, improving agent efficiency, and applying governance controls to manage cost at scale.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="get-started">Get started</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  This series will continue over the coming weeks, going one level deeper on each subsequent move: how to&nbsp;optimize&nbsp;the request at runtime, how to build agents that use tokens efficiently, and how to govern the spend as you scale.&nbsp;Each post pairs the thinking with the Foundry capabilities that make it real.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">You don&#8217;t have to wait to start. The capabilities behind this framework are live in <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> today: </p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Learn more about the ways to <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/control-plane/how-to-optimize-cost-performance" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">optimize model cost and performance</a> in Microsoft Foundry.  </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Watch the <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mB0IyELzjRg">token economics episode on&nbsp;<strong>Microsoft Mechanics</strong></a> for a hands-on look at the levers in action. </li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Follow along as the series&nbsp;unfolds and&nbsp;bring the four questions to your next review.</p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="build-an-ai-investment-strategy-that-scales">Build an AI investment strategy that scales</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Foundry is the enterprise AI platform to build, ground, and govern AI apps and agents at scale.</p>

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft has been named a <a href="https://www.gartner.com/reprints/?id=00ThR000008k1rGUAQ&amp;ct=260804&amp;st=sb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Leader in the inaugural 2026 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for AI-Augmented Code Modernization Tools</a>, which recognizes software solutions that use specialized AI agents, generative AI, and deterministic analysis to accelerate the transformation of legacy systems. We are proud of our placement as a Leader in this first edition of the report.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="cloud-native-modernization">Cloud-native modernization</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft’s conviction from the start has been that modernization should become one of the fastest, most trusted, and most predictable workflows organizations undertake, and agentic AI is what finally makes that possible. For years, modernization and innovation competed for the same budget, and modernization usually lost. Now the math has changed: modernization is no longer the tax teams pay before they can innovate, it is the fastest path to it, and the gateway to AI. Eliminating decades of technical debt is what lets teams embed intelligence directly into the proven, high-value systems that already run their business. This modern foundation turns the assets they depend on most into the platform for what comes next.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.gartner.com/reprints/?id=00ThR000008k1rGUAQ&amp;ct=260804&amp;st=sb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Read the report</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">None of this is on the horizon; it is happening now. Modernization is shifting from a multi-year program into something teams finish in days or weeks.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a846551d18cb&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a846551d18cb" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-1024x576.webp" alt="Gartner graph, including completeness of vision and ability to execute." class="wp-image-53126 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-1024x576.webp 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-300x169.webp 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-768x432.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-1536x864.webp 1536w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-809x455.webp 809w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1.webp 1920w" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-1024x576.webp"><button

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, organizations are not just moving to the cloud or reducing technical debt, they are fundamentally rethinking their applications, their data, and their infrastructure because they know the decisions they make today will determine whether they are AI-ready tomorrow. Every modernization decision is now an AI decision.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft Azure was built for this moment. It provides the agentic foundation and engine for modernization and AI transformation, along with the governance and trust enterprises require, so modernization lowers security, data, and operational risk as much as it accelerates AI readiness. With agentic tooling and an end-to-end modernization solution powered by <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/application-and-database-modernization/#Products-3" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-in/products/github/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">GitHub Copilot</a> and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/copilot" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Copilot</a>, Microsoft is accelerating the shift, not just leading it.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a846551d2820&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a846551d2820" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-1024x575.webp" alt="Chart visualization of Agentic Modernization on Azure." class="wp-image-53127 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-1024x575.webp 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-300x169.webp 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-768x432.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-1536x863.webp 1536w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-809x455.webp 809w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1.webp 1920w" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-1024x575.webp"><button

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-problem-we-set-out-to-solve">The problem we set out to solve</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Every enterprise carries code it cannot afford to rewrite and cannot afford to ignore. Legacy .NET Framework applications, Java runtimes, aging dependencies, and accumulated security debt power critical business processes while also limiting an organization’s ability to move forward. For decades, modernization meant large teams, months of manual effort, and a real risk of breaking what already works. As a result, it got deferred, repeatedly, while technical debt compounded and the distance to the cloud grew.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We feel Gartner frames the opportunity perfectly: software engineering leaders can meaningfully reduce the time, effort, and risk of modernization by using AI-powered tools rather than manual approaches. That distance, between the cost of standing still and the cost of moving, is exactly where we put our focus. The goal was never to help teams modernize a little faster. It was to fundamentally change the economics of modernization.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="how-it-works">How it works</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The engine behind this is <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/application-and-database-modernization/#Products-3" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-in/products/github/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">GitHub Copilot</a> modernization, an agentic, end-to-end approach that helps teams assess, upgrade, and migrate applications to Azure, with AI handling repetitive tasks while developers remain firmly in control.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We deliver it through two layers that work together. The modernization agent, available through the Modernize CLI, enables architects and application owners to orchestrate assessment, planning, and framework-upgrade automation across many applications at once, then delivers robust application-specific plans to developers. Inside the IDE, developers use GitHub Copilot to execute those plans: upgrading runtimes and frameworks, migrating dependencies to Azure services, generating infrastructure-as-code, and deploying to production. Assessment, planning, transformation, validation, and deployment become one continuous, guided journey rather than a dozen disconnected steps.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">What we chose not to automate matters just as much, and that is human judgment. Every recommendation is transparent, every change is reviewable, and every step is validated against your own tests and pipelines. This is modernization at machine speed, with the accountability enterprises require.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="where-we-believe-we-stand-apart">Where we believe we stand apart</h2>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Breadth: </strong>Github Copilot modernization spans the languages that actually run the enterprise—.NET, Java, C++, and Python—rather than a single stack, so platform teams can standardize on one approach.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Repeatable expertise: </strong>Predefined tasks capture best practices for common migration patterns, such as adopting managed identities, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/key-vault" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Key Vault</a>, Blob and File storage, or <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-in/security/business/identity-access/microsoft-entra-id" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Entra ID</a>. Then custom skills let teams turn their own proven code changes into reusable patterns, applying them consistently across projects and ensuring institutional knowledge stays within the organization.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Trust built in: </strong>Security is part of the workflow, not a phase bolted on at the end. Copilot validates builds, migrates unit tests, scans for CVEs after upgrades, and applies fixes in agent mode—strengthening your security posture as you modernize, not after.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Enterprise readiness</strong>: Modernizing one application is a project; modernizing an entire estate is a business imperative. What makes the difference at scale is consistency: the patterns that work become reusable tasks and skills any team can apply again, so a proven approach spreads across the portfolio instead of being reinvented app by app. It is what turns modernization from a string of one-off wins into a repeatable, governable practice.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>The destination, not just the journey: </strong>Modernization is continuous. Applications land on Azure—<a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/app-service" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/app-service" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure App Service</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/container-apps" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/container-apps" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Container Apps</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/kubernetes-service" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/kubernetes-service" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Kubernetes Service</a>—and become ready to tap into the full system from silicon to software. This includes <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a>’s catalog of more than 11,000 models and <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" id="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft IQ</a>, the enterprise intelligence layer that grounds AI agents in your organization&#8217;s own data and knowledge. Teams don’t just retire technical debt; they unlock what’s next.</li>
</ul>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-results-that-make-it-real">The results that make it real</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The numbers coming back from teams already doing this work are what change the conversation. Customers using GitHub Copilot modernization capabilities <a href="https://github.com/solutions/use-case/app-modernization" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">report up to 70% less time spent on migration efforts and 50% less effort to upgrade applications</a>. Some have moved more than 500,000 lines of code in a matter of weeks, work that once would have consumed quarters. Adoption tracks the same story: <a href="https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=vscjava.migrate-java-to-azure" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">more than 1.4 million developers have already installed the experience</a>.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote is-style-default has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">GitHub Copilot’s modernization agent significantly saved time on our Java modernization effort from days down to mere hours at SAP Labs. It runs seamlessly at scale, significantly reducing manual complications for our developers. The modernization agent is now an essential pillar of our modernization effort.</p>
<cite>—Dr Richard Cai, Managing Director, SAP Labs China</cite></blockquote>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="this-is-the-starting-line">This is the starting line</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Being named a Leader in this Magic Quadrant is an honor for us, and candidly it is an obligation. The teams that adopt AI-powered modernization now will spend the next decade building on modern, secure foundations, in the cloud or hybrid, while others are still untangling the past. We intend to keep widening that gap with deeper language and framework coverage, richer agentic automation, and an ever-shorter path from legacy code to production on Azure. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">To every customer and partner who has modernized alongside us, thank you. You shaped this, and you are the reason for the recognition. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">If you are ready to see what is possible, explore agentic modernization and start with a single application this week. You can also <a href="https://www.gartner.com/reprints/?id=00ThR000008k1rGUAQ&amp;ct=260804&amp;st=sb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">read a complimentary copy of the report to see why Microsoft was named a Leader</a>.</p>

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Every day, customers trust Microsoft Databases to power their most critical applications, business processes, and AI-powered experiences. Continuous customer feedback provides valuable insights into how these technologies perform in production and where we should continue investing.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">I’m honored to share that Microsoft Databases have received multiple 2026 PeerSpot Customer Choice / Tech Leader recognitions based on feedback from real practitioners using these technologies in production environments. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This year, <a href="https://customerchoiceawards.peerspot.com/awards/microsoft" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">PeerSpot recognized</a>:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>SQL Server</strong> as a Tech Leader in <strong>Database Management Systems (DBMS)</strong> (#1) and <strong>Relational Database Tools</strong> (#1) </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Azure SQL Database</strong> as a Tech Leader in <strong>Database as a Service (DBaaS)</strong> (#2)</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Azure Database for PostgreSQL</strong> as a Tech Leader in <strong>Open Source Databases</strong> (#1) </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Azure Cosmos DB</strong> as a Tech Leader in <strong>Managed NoSQL Databases</strong> (#1), <strong>NoSQL Databases</strong> (#1), and <strong>Vector Databases</strong> (#1) </li>
</ul>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://customerchoiceawards.peerspot.com/awards/microsoft" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Learn more about our PeerSpot awards</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Customer feedback across Microsoft Databases consistently highlights five themes: reliability, scalability, operational simplicity, developer productivity, and AI readiness. These are the same priorities shaping our investments across <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en/sql-server" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">SQL Server</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/azure-sql/database/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure SQL Database</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/cosmos-db/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Cosmos DB</a>, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/postgresql/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Database for PostgreSQL</a>. The PeerSpot recognition reinforces that customers see value not only in the performance of our databases today, but also in how they are helping organizations prepare for the next generation of AI-powered applications.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-customers-consistently-value"><strong>What customers consistently value</strong> </h2>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="sql-server-trusted-reliability-for-mission-critical-workloads">SQL Server: Trusted reliability for mission-critical workloads </h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For SQL Server customers, reliability, stability, and long-term data stewardship remain foundational requirements. Organizations continue to rely on SQL Server to support business-critical applications, analytics, and increasingly AI-enabled workflows. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Hemanth Reddy Vakiti, a data engineer at Cognizant,&nbsp;wrote that <a href="https://www.peerspot.com/products/sql-server-reviews?review_id=11107492" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>“The backup and recovery allow us to easily access previous 10 to 15 years of data”</strong></a>, citing SQL Server’s ability to handle large datasets and support secure role-based access.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Another SQL Server reviewer,&nbsp;Isseo Martinez&nbsp;from&nbsp;eGlobal,&nbsp;called out availability and stability, saying&nbsp;<a href="https://www.peerspot.com/products/sql-server-reviews?review_id=9505899" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>&#8220;We&nbsp;never have concerns about the database.”</strong></a> For&nbsp;many organizations, that confidence is the foundation for business-critical applications and long-term modernization strategies.&nbsp;</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/sql-server/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Explore SQL Server 2025</a></div>
</div>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="azure-sql-database-simplifying-modernization-with-cloud-scale-sql">Azure SQL Database: Simplifying modernization with cloud-scale SQL </h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Customers regularly tell us they want the reliability and familiarity of SQL Server with the operational simplicity of a fully managed cloud service. Azure SQL Database helps organizations modernize applications, reduce infrastructure management, and scale with confidence. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Fredrick Weinshenk, a cloud engineer at healthcare IT firm Med Tech Solutions, wrote that <a href="https://www.peerspot.com/products/microsoft-azure-sql-database-reviews?review_id=7643726" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>“The ease of deployment and scalability are significant reasons we push our larger clients to Azure,”</strong></a> and described the platform as “reliable” and “solid.”</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">That feedback reflects a recurring need we hear from customers: modernize infrastructure, reduce operational burden, and retain the SQL skills, tools, and application patterns their teams already trust. </p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/azure-sql/database/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Explore Azure SQL Database</a></div>
</div>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="azure-database-for-postgresql-open-source-flexibility-with-enterprise-reliability">Azure Database for PostgreSQL: Open-source flexibility with enterprise reliability </h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Customers choose Azure Database for PostgreSQL because it combines the innovation of the PostgreSQL ecosystem with the operational benefits of a managed service. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Wesley Haakman, a principal architect at IT service provider Intercept, using the service across hundreds of customer scenarios, shared that “[the] organization’s database uptime has improved” and highlighted the ease of integration with other Azure services.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Sathish Palanisamy, data engineer at a large transportation company, said <a href="https://www.peerspot.com/products/azure-database-for-postgresql-reviews?review_id=10068732" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>&#8220;We&#8217;ve used Azure Database for PostgreSQL for four years, benefiting from its performance, scalability, ease of integration, and support, especially during migration from Oracle, while also reducing costs and enabling faster, secure, AI-driven innovation in our airline operations.&#8221;</strong></a></p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">These reviews highlight the value customers place on flexibility, modernization, and a clear path to AI-powered innovation.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/postgresql/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Explore Azure Database for PostgreSQL</a></div>
</div>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="azure-cosmos-db-global-scale-and-performance-for-modern-applications">Azure Cosmos DB: Global scale and performance for modern applications </h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Customers building modern cloud-native and AI-powered applications consistently point to Azure Cosmos DB’s combination of low latency, elastic scalability, flexible data models, and search performance. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Kunal Mukerjee, former vice president of technology strategy at Docusign, said <a href="https://www.peerspot.com/product_reviews/microsoft-azure-cosmos-db-review-7643748-by-kunal-mukerjee?cop=p9665" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>“Cosmos DB has improved search result quality, throughput, and query latency. There are trade-offs to finding the sweet spot among all of these. Having a NoSQL solution that can do that in a 100 percent Azure shop is the best fit we could want.”</strong></a></p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Another customer, Yoni Nijs, CTO of Zero Friction, described using Azure Cosmos DB serverless for a highly unpredictable consumer portal and provisioned throughput with autoscale for a back-office application, noting that the configuration was <a href="https://www.peerspot.com/products/microsoft-azure-cosmos-db-reviews?review_id=7316875" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>“&#8230; very scalable and still cost-effective.”</strong></a></p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">These are exactly the patterns&nbsp;AI&nbsp;applications demand:&nbsp;responsive global systems that can adapt to changing demand without sacrificing performance or cost efficiency.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/cosmos-db/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Explore Azure Cosmos DB</a></div>
</div>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity"/>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Taken together, these reviews reinforce a clear message: organizations want databases that are not only secure and scalable, but also ready for the next generation of intelligent applications. </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="turning-customer-feedback-into-innovation"><strong>Turning customer feedback into innovation</strong> </h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The themes customers highlighted are the same themes guiding our investments across the Microsoft Databases portfolio. Our goal is not simply to add new features, but to help customers solve real challenges: modernizing existing applications, building AI-powered experiences, reducing operational complexity, and scaling with confidence.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="sql-server">SQL Server</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Relational databases continue to power many of the world&#8217;s most critical applications. With SQL Server 2025, we&#8217;re helping customers extend those applications into the AI era without changing the platforms, skills, or tools they already trust. Native vector support, in-database embedding generation, semantic search, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) capabilities make it easier to build intelligent applications while keeping data secure, governed, and close to operational workloads. </p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="azure-sql-database">Azure SQL Database</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With&nbsp;Azure SQL Database,&nbsp;customers get a&nbsp;fully&nbsp;managed&nbsp;cloud-native SQL solution for modernizing and building AI applications.&nbsp;Azure&nbsp;SQL Database Hyperscale helps applications scale with demand and grow as needs evolve, while automatic tuning and elastic resource management improve performance and cost efficiency.&nbsp;Customers&nbsp;can build AI-powered experiences on operational data with AI retrieval and secure agent connectivity,&nbsp;with&nbsp;integrations&nbsp;across&nbsp;key AI and analytics solutions like&nbsp;<a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft&nbsp;Foundry</a>&nbsp;and&nbsp;<a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft&nbsp;Fabric</a>.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This helps&nbsp;their&nbsp;organization&nbsp;build a&nbsp;direct path from&nbsp;business-critical data&nbsp;to intelligent applications,&nbsp;without compromising the security, governance, and performance they expect from Microsoft&#8217;s SQL platforms.&nbsp;</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="azure-database-for-postgresql">Azure Database for PostgreSQL</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For PostgreSQL on Azure, our investments focus on helping customers modernize faster, accelerate AI development, and run mission-critical workloads with greater confidence. Highlights include the public preview of Azure HorizonDB, bringing AI-native capabilities such as integrated vector search, AI functions, AI pipelines, and model management to PostgreSQL; new migration and upgrade innovations including AI-assisted Oracle-to-PostgreSQL migration tooling; and performance improvements such as Premium SSD v2, cascading read replicas, and new high-performance infrastructure options for Azure Database for PostgreSQL. These innovations are helping organizations adopt AI capabilities while continuing to benefit from PostgreSQL&#8217;s open-source ecosystem. </p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="azure-cosmos-db">Azure Cosmos DB</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For customers building AI-native applications, Azure Cosmos DB investments focus on helping developers move faster while simplifying operations at scale. Recent innovations include enhanced vector and hybrid search, semantic reranking, agent-focused development tools, Global Secondary Indexes, per-partition automatic failover, richer change feed capabilities, and improvements to local development through the Linux Emulator. Together, these investments help developers build responsive, AI-powered applications faster while maintaining performance, resilience, and operational efficiency. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Across the portfolio, our goal is consistent: help customers build applications faster, operate them more efficiently, and prepare for a future where AI is deeply integrated into every application experience. </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="thank-you-to-our-customers-and-community"><strong>Thank you to our customers and community</strong> </h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">These PeerSpot recognitions belong first and foremost to our customers and community. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Every review, product suggestion, support interaction, preview program discussion, and customer conversation helps shape the direction of Microsoft Databases. Your feedback helps other technology leaders make informed decisions, and it helps our teams build better products. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">On behalf of everyone across the Microsoft Databases organization, thank you for your trust, your partnership, and your willingness to share your experiences. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">I encourage you to read what your peers are saying, and, if Microsoft Databases are helping your organization innovate, consider sharing your own review.</p>

		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Steve Sweetman]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 23 Jul 2026 18:30:00 +0000</pubDate>

		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=52857</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>AT&#38;T processed approximately one trillion tokens while developing OTel2.0 using Microsoft Foundry Managed Compute, open AI models, and AMD and NVIDIA GPU infrastructure. Discover how flexible model choice and scalable infrastructure are enabling production-scale telecom AI.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/att-and-microsoft-scale-trillion-token-workloads-with-microsoft-foundry-and-amd/">AT&amp;T and Microsoft scale trillion-token workloads with Microsoft Foundry and AMD</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="first-of-its-kind-telecom-ai-deployment">First-of-its-kind telecom AI deployment</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Telecommunications organizations are increasingly looking to AI to help teams navigate highly specialized domains, but generic models often lack the industry-specific knowledge needed to understand telecom networks, standards, and operations. To address that gap, AT&amp;T created their Open Telco (OTel) models, the next generation of telecom-focused AI designed to bring deeper telecommunications expertise into AI systems. Building OTel2.0 required more than training a large language model, it reflected a broader issue many organizations face: how to build domain-specific AI systems at scale while balancing cost, performance, and operational complexity. Cost management quickly became a key consideration. To continue advancing telecom-focused AI, AT&amp;T needed a platform capable of supporting OTel2.0 development at an entirely new scale.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Where teams previously had to own and manage deployments, infrastructure, and the associated operational overhead, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/managed-compute-overview">Foundry Managed Compute</a> provided a more streamlined way to access dedicated graphics processing unit (GPU) capacity. This transformation requires more than powerful models; it requires the ability to scale without compromising cost, flexibility, or performance.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.gsma.com/newsroom/press-release/gsma-launches-open-telco-ai-to-accelerate-development-of-telco%E2%80%91grade-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer noreferrer noopener">Learn how OTel2.0 scales telecom AI<strong style="font-family: inherit;font-size: 15.008px;font-style: inherit;letter-spacing: 0.30016px;text-transform: inherit"></strong></a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Using <a href="https://devblogs.microsoft.com/foundry/announcing-foundry-managed-compute/">Microsoft Foundry Managed Compute</a>, AT&amp;T was able to experiment across multiple open models, optimize workloads across different GPU architectures, and process massive volumes of telecom data all within a unified platform. The result was an AI development environment capable of supporting trillions of tokens while giving teams the flexibility to iterate, optimize, and innovate faster.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="model-choice-meets-infrastructure-flexibility">Model choice meets infrastructure flexibility </h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Building OTel2.0 required flexibility across both models and infrastructure. Rather than standardizing on a single model, AT&amp;T adopted a multi open-model strategy. Open models were central to AT&amp;T’s approach because they provided the flexibility to work with approved telecom data, tailor the workflow for domain-specific model development, and support large-scale experimentation with greater control over cost and deployment strategy. Through <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a>, the team deployed several models from the Hugging Face collection, including <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/phi" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Phi-4</a>, OSS-120B, and Gemma-4, to support different stages of development, from synthetic data generation and data preparation to reasoning-intensive workloads and broader model development efforts. Phi-4 played a significant role in this process, processing more than 700 billion tokens a month as part of the broader data preparation and training workflow for OTel2.0.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Every company in the world needs to build its own AI, and that is only possible with open models and open source. AT&amp;T is championing this vision, building on open models like Phi-4 and Gemma, and giving OTel back to the community as a telecom AI foundation others can build upon. Microsoft Foundry makes this practical at scale, bringing the latest open models from the Hugging Face collection together with AMD and NVIDIA GPUs in one place, so teams can pick the right model and the right hardware, then deploy in hours instead of weeks.</p>
<cite>—Jeff Boudier, Vice President of Product, Hugging Face</cite></blockquote>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://www.gsma.com/newsroom/press-release/gsma-launches-open-telco-ai-to-accelerate-development-of-telco%E2%80%91grade-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Developing OTel2.0</a> also required infrastructure capable of operating at telecom scale. AT&amp;T used approximately <strong>530 GPUs</strong> through Microsoft Foundry Managed Compute spanning multiple GPU architectures including <strong>430 AMD Instinct™ MI300X GPUs</strong>. This heterogenous approach gave AT&amp;T more flexibility in how models were deployed and&nbsp;optimized as requirements evolved.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-table aligncenter is-style-stripes"><table class="has-white-color has-electric-blue-400-background-color has-text-color has-background has-link-color has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Model</th><th class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Example workload</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Phi-4</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Around 700B tokens a month for <br>data preparation and synthetic <br>data generation</td></tr><tr><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">OSS 120B</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Higher-reasoning workloads </td></tr><tr><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Gemma 4</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">OTel2.0 development workflows</td></tr></tbody></table><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Table 1:&nbsp;Explains what&nbsp;open source&nbsp;models were used and how</figcaption></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This flexibility illustrates a broader trend across AI development. Organizations increasingly need platforms that allow them to choose the right model for the job, optimize for cost and performance, and scale workloads without rebuilding operational environments. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> brings model choice, infrastructure flexibility, governance, and operational scale together in a unified platform that supports those requirements.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Start building with Microsoft Foundry</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Beyond flexibility and cost, deployment speed is a critical factor for many AI initiatives. As workloads expand and new models are evaluated, the ability to access GPU capacity quickly enables teams to move from experimentation to execution faster without lengthy provisioning cycles. With Foundry Managed Compute, AT&amp;T could deploy and scale models in days rather than waiting weeks for infrastructure to become available, helping accelerate development timelines and maintain momentum across OTel2.0 development.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="optimizing-cost-without-limiting-innovation">Optimizing cost without limiting innovation  </h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  As AI workloads grow, economics become as important as model performance. For AT&amp;T,  one of the primary objectives was to lower AI model consumption costs while continuing to drive meaningful business value through AI-powered innovation. By using open models on <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/managed-compute-overview">Microsoft Foundry Managed Compute</a>, AT&amp;T was able to support large-scale data preparation and model development using a different economic model built around dedicated GPU infrastructure and open-model flexibility.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The impact became clear at scale. In support of OTel2.0, AT&amp;T processed approximately 1T tokens, consisting of raw documents from GSMA supplemented by synthetic data generated. Generating the data using open-source models like Phi-4, served by Microsoft&#8217;s Foundry Managed Compute, saved tens of millions of dollars versus using frontier models. This allowed teams to invest in larger-scale experimentation and development while maintaining a focus on business value and operational efficiency.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-table aligncenter is-style-stripes has-body-medium-font-size"><table class="has-white-color has-electric-blue-400-background-color has-text-color has-background has-link-color has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th>Metric</th><th class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Value</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>OTel 1.0 Downloads</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Over 25M</td></tr><tr><td>GPUs Used Through Foundry Managed Compute</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">About 530</td></tr><tr><td>Tokens Processed for OTel2.0</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">About 1T</td></tr><tr><td>Tokens Trained for OTel2.0</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">About&nbsp;400&nbsp;B</td></tr><tr><td>Models used to train OTel</td><td class="has-text-align-left" data-align="left">Phi-4, OSS 120B, Gemma 4</td></tr></tbody></table><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Table 2: Quick facts about the OTel model family and metrics around what was used to build OTel2.0&nbsp;</figcaption></figure>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When you are processing hundreds of billions of tokens, infrastructure becomes part of the problem you solve. Foundry Managed Compute gave us access to GPU capacity at scale so our teams could focus on advancing OTel2.0 instead of managing infrastructure.</p>
<cite>—Mark Austin, Vice President, Data Science and AI at AT&amp;T</cite></blockquote>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  At this scale, infrastructure is no longer simply a <a id="post-52857-_Int_JNQm0EQ5"></a>deployment consideration. It <a id="post-52857-_Int_PdbJnM1r"></a>becomes a strategic component of AI development.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="accelerating-the-next-wave-of-production-scale-ai">Accelerating the next wave of production-scale AI</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">OTel 2.0 demonstrates how organizations can combine open models, scalable infrastructure, and domain expertise to build production-ready AI systems. By matching different models to different workloads and optimizing infrastructure for cost and performance, AT&amp;T was able to process trillions of tokens while maintaining operational efficiency.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As organizations move from AI experimentation to production deployment, they increasingly need the flexibility to choose the right models, optimize infrastructure, and scale efficiently. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/concepts/managed-compute-overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry Managed Compute</a> help support that transition by bringing those capabilities together in a unified platform.</p>

<li class="wp-block-list-item"><a href="https://ai.azure.com/catalog/models/farbodtavakkoli--otel-2.0-llm-31b-it?search=otel" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">OTel2.0 models</a> are now available.</li>

		<item>
		<title>Azure Databricks delivers proven business value</title>
		<link>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/azure-databricks-delivers-proven-business-value/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Jason Pereira and Anavi Nahar]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 15 Jul 2026 15:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[AI + machine learning]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Analytics]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Microsoft OneLake]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=52752</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Microsoft Azure Databricks delivers the first-party advantage of Databricks on Microsoft—and for customers, that advantage shows up as real, measurable value. It is the same Databricks platform your teams already know, co-engineered with Microsoft and delivered as a native Azure service, so it fits naturally into the Microsoft tools, identity, and governance your organization already runs.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/azure-databricks-delivers-proven-business-value/">Azure Databricks delivers proven business value</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure Databricks</a> delivers the first-party advantage of Databricks on Microsoft—and for customers, that advantage shows up as real, measurable value. It is the same Databricks platform your teams already know, co-engineered with Microsoft and delivered as a native <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure service</a>, so it fits naturally into the Microsoft tools, identity, and governance your organization already runs.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Discover Azure Databricks</a></div>
</div>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">
  The advantage is built-in, not bolted on. Microsoft and Databricks co-engineer the service, share one integration roadmap across the Microsoft data and AI stack, and align go-to-market so you get one motion, one bill, and one support path. For technical teams, that means deeper native integration and stronger performance. For the business, it means lower cost, less risk, and faster time to value.
</p>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">The strategic partnership drives an accelerated integration roadmap and continuous optimization for improved performance; however, decision-makers constantly ask about what business value all of this translates to. To address this key question, Microsoft commissioned a <a href="https://tei.forrester.com/go/Microsoft/Databricks/?lang=en-us" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Forrester Total Economic Impact™ study</a> of <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a>. It found that a composite organization based on interviewed customers realized a three-year <strong>331% return on investment</strong>, <strong>$58.1 million in net present value</strong>, and recovered its investment in <strong>less than six months</strong>.</p>



<div class="section wp-block-bloginabox-theme-section has-global-padding is-layout-constrained wp-block-bloginabox-theme-section-is-layout-constrained">
	
<div
	class="section__background alignfull is-layout-constrained"
	>

				<div class="section-background-image-wrapper section-background-image-wrapper--light section-background-image-wrapper--desktop alignfull section--emerald-dusk" style="opacity: 100%">
					</div>
	
				<div class="section-background-image-wrapper section-background-image-wrapper--dark section-background-image-wrapper--desktop alignfull" style="opacity: 100%">
					</div>
	
		
		</div>
<div class="section__content alignfull is-layout-constrained">
	

<figure class="wp-block-table aligncenter has-body-large-font-size"><table class="has-white-color has-electric-blue-200-background-color has-text-color has-background has-link-color has-fixed-layout"><tbody><tr><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>331%</strong><br>Return on investment&nbsp;</td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>$58.1M</strong><br>Net present value</td><td class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center"><strong>&lt; 6 months</strong><br>Payback period</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>

</div>
</div>



<p class="has-text-align-center has-body-medium-font-size wp-block-paragraph"><em>Commissioned study conducted by Forrester Consulting on behalf of Microsoft, June 2026. Results are over three years and represent a composite organization based on interviewed customers and may not be typical; actual results will vary.</em>
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-the-study-found">What the study found</h2>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Forrester interviewed <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a> customers and built a composite organization to model the impact: a $6 billion company in a regulated industry, running about 10 petabytes of data.</p>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">
  Before Azure Databricks, its data estate was fragmented and expensive. It was unreliable at scale and hard to govern. Afterward, the results were clear: $75.6 million in benefits against $17.5 million in cost over three years translating to $58.1 million in net present value.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The value came from four places:
</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>$39.0 million—data and analytics teams’ productivity</strong>. Teams handled more work without adding people, with measured gains of 15% to 25%. As a Vice President of data services at a healthcare organization put it: “<em>…we’re doing more work with the same size of the team</em>.”</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>$19.9 million—lower infrastructure costs</strong>. Elastic, pay-as-you-go compute replaced overprovisioned hardware.</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>$11.4 million—better data platform resiliency</strong>. Managed operations meant fewer outages and no custom disaster recovery to build.</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>$5.4 million—retired legacy software and redeployed DBAs</strong>.<strong>&nbsp;</strong>Consolidating databases and Extract, Load, Transform (ETL) tools eliminated third-party licenses, and managed operations freed database administrators for higher-value work.</li>
</ul>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Forrester listed more benefits it didn’t put a price on: native Azure services integration, faster insights, wider access to data, and governance through Unity Catalog. That’s where the return starts.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://tei.forrester.com/go/Microsoft/Databricks" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Model your own numbers with the Azure Databricks ROI estimator</a></div>
</div>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large"><a href="https://tei.forrester.com/go/Microsoft/Databricks/?lang=en-us" target="_blank" rel=" noreferrer noopener"><img fetchpriority="high" decoding="async" width="791" height="1024" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-791x1024.jpg" alt="The Total Economic Impact of Microsoft Azure Databricks infographic" class="wp-image-52760" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-791x1024.jpg 791w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-232x300.jpg 232w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-768x994.jpg 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-1187x1536.jpg 1187w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic-1583x2048.jpg 1583w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/TEI-of-ADB-infographic.jpg 1700w" sizes="(max-width: 791px) 100vw, 791px" /></a></figure>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="where-the-value-comes-from">Where the value comes from</h2>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Those returns come down to one thing. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a> is a true first-party Azure service, co-engineered by Microsoft and Databricks, it plugs into the tools your teams already use. That removes the extra data copies, tooling, and integration work that raise costs elsewhere.</p>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">A great example is the <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/genie-one/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Databricks Genie</a> integration with <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/cowork" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Copilot Cowork</a>. You can add context of your business and build on that intelligence into the tools your teams already use with this integration. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/genie-ui/genie" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Genie</a> lets anyone question the lakehouse in plain language—now inside <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/integrations/msft-teams" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Teams</a>, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/integrations/msft-m365-copilot" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft 365 Copilot</a>, and more recently in <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/azure-databricks-at-databricks-data--ai-summit-2026-updates-and-new-announcement/4528388" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Copilot Cowork</a>, where it grounds tasks in trusted data through Genie Ontology. Every answer is scoped by Unity Catalog to exactly what each user is permitted to see, so intelligence reaches the flow of work without loosening governance.</p>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">
  The same depth runs across the rest of the platform:
</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Identity and governance</strong>: <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/general-availability-automatic-identity-management-aim-for-entra-id-on-azure-dat/4452206" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Automatic Identity Management for Entra ID</a> syncs users into <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a>. Unity Catalog and <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/security/business/microsoft-purview" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Microsoft Purview</a> govern the rest.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Microsoft Power BI and Microsoft 365</strong>: <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/power-platform/products/power-bi">Power BI</a> reads your data directly and now <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/closing-the-loop-interactive-write-back-from-power-bi-to-azure-databricks/4442999">writes back to it</a>. A new Excel add-in brings governed data into spreadsheets; a SharePoint connector streams files into Delta tables, and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/ai-bi/admin/teams-subscriptions">Teams notifications</a> deliver alerts where teams work. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/metric-views/">Metric views</a> keep business logic consistent across all of them.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>AI and agents</strong>: Beyond chatting with your data, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/genie-ui/genie" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Genie</a> connects to <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/azure-databricks-genie-integration-with-copilot-studio-and-microsoft-foundry-is-/4471087" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Copilot Studio and Microsoft Foundry</a>, and a single Model Context Protocol (MCP) connection lets <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/analyticsonazure/building-ai-apps-and-agents-with-azure-databricks-copilot-studio-and-github-copi/4524065" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Copilot Studio and GitHub Copilot agents</a> reason over an entire Azure Databricks workspace. <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/azure-databricks-lakebase-is-now-generally-available/4498779" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Database Lakebase</a> gives agents a serverless Postgres engine, and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/admin/workspace/serverless-workspaces" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">serverless workspaces</a> get them started fast.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Microsoft OneLake</strong>: <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/databricks/query-federation/onelake" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">OneLake catalog federation</a> lets Azure Databricks query OneLake data directly, with no pipelines or copies. You can also <a href="https://community.fabric.microsoft.com/t5/Fabric-Updates-Blog/Extending-interoperability-Azure-Databricks-can-now-store-Unity/ba-p/5199741" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">store Unity Catalog tables in OneLake</a>, alongside Azure Data Lake Storage.</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Customer data</strong>: <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/azure-databricks-at-databricks-data--ai-summit-2026-updates-and-new-announcement/4528388">CustomerLake</a>, a new Agentic Customer Data platform, builds Customer 360 profiles and runs campaigns inside the lakehouse where the data and governance already live.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-list-item"><strong>Enterprise systems</strong>: <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/azure-databricks/sap-business-data-cloud-connect-with-azure-databricks-is-now-generally-available/4459490">SAP Business Data Cloud Connect</a> brings SAP data into the lakehouse. It’s the same model helping industries like <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/telecommunications/2026/02/24/microsoft-accelerates-telecom-return-on-intelligence-with-a-unified-trusted-ai-platform/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">industries like telecom</a> turn AI into returns.&nbsp;</li>
</ul>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">
  These are the integrations that Forrester valued but didn’t price separately however, they are critical factors driving productivity and cost benefits that were quantified.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="backed-by-independent-benchmarks">Backed by independent benchmarks</h2>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Value also depends on speed, and that’s been tested. Principled Technologies, an independent firm, ran an industry-standard, TPC-DS-like <a href="https://www.principledtechnologies.com/clients/reports/Microsoft/Azure-Databricks-competitive-0725/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">decision-support benchmark</a> on a 10-terabyte dataset. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a> completed a single query stream in up to 21.1% less time than Databricks on AWS (with autoscale disabled) and ran four concurrent query streams more than nine minutes faster.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="what-it-means-for-you">What it means for you</h2>



<p class="has-body-large-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Choosing a data and AI platform is a long-term decision, and with <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/databricks/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Databricks</a> the pieces reinforce each other. The integration drives the savings Forrester measured. The performance keeps those gains steady as usage grows. And it all rests on one foundation: a first-party partnership that puts Microsoft and Databricks engineering, roadmap, and support behind your data estate. The value isn’t a claim, it’s been measured: a three-year 331% return, with payback in under six months. It’s why so many teams choose to run their lakehouse on Azure Databricks.</p>

		<title>GPT-5.6 now available in Microsoft Foundry </title>
		<link>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/gpt-5-6-now-available-in-microsoft-foundry/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Tina Schuchman]]></dc:creator>
